If its initial value is positive, applying the chain rule to its reciprocal while it remains positive and integrating (S.8.5) gives the following bound; if it reaches zero, nonnegativity and monotonicity keep it there. Thus,

$$
J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ( t ) ) \leq \frac { J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ( 0 ) ) } { 1 + \kappa _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \mathrm { a c } } t J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ( 0 ) ) } .\tag{S.8.6}
$$

Equilibrium set and fnite-time convergence of the actor ODE. To verify the uniform one-block Lyapunov contraction condition required by Theorem 8.4, we first identify the equilibrium set of the projected actor ODE and then show that every trajectory reaches this set within a uniform finite time. Define

$$
K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } : = c ( s , a ) + \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } \sum _ { s ^ { \prime } } P ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ( s ^ { \prime } ) - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ( s )
$$

and

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { { \mathscr E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } : = \{ \vartheta \in \Theta _ { \mathrm { a c } } : \vartheta ( s , a ) = B _ { \mathrm { a c } } \mathrm { ~ i f ~ } K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } < 0 , \smallskip } \\ & { \qquad \vartheta ( s , a ) = - B _ { \mathrm { a c } } \mathrm { ~ i f ~ } K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } > 0 \} . } \end{array}
$$

Coordinates with $K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } = 0$ are unrestricted. For each state s and $\eta \in [ - B _ { \mathrm { a c } } , B _ { \mathrm { a c } } ] ^ { | A | }$ , define

$$
N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \eta ) : = \sum _ { a } e ^ { \eta ( a ) } K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } .
$$

Because $V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ is the fixed point of the restricted Bellman operator and the softmax image of the logit box is exactly $\mathcal { P } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$

$$
0 = [ T _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ] ( s ) - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ( s ) = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { \eta \in [ - B _ { \mathrm { a c } } , B _ { \mathrm { a c } } ] ^ { | { \cal A } | } } \frac { N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \eta ) } { Z _ { s } ( \eta ) } .
$$

The claim $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ) = 0 \iff \vartheta \in \mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ identifies both the hitting target and, as shown below, the actor ODE equilibria. First suppose $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ) = 0$ . Since $V _ { \vartheta } - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ is componentwise nonnegative, this implies $V _ { \vartheta } = V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ . The policy-evaluation fixed-point equation then gives

$$
N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \vartheta ( s , \cdot ) ) = Z _ { s } ( \vartheta ( s , \cdot ) ) \big \{ [ T _ { \vartheta } V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ] ( s ) - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ( s ) \big \} = 0 .
$$

If $K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } > 0$ but $\vartheta ( s , a ) > - B _ { \mathrm { a c } } ,$ lowering only that logit makes $N _ { s } ^ { \star }$ strictly negative. Since $Z _ { s } > 0$ 2 this contradicts the preceding restricted Bellman minimum. Likewise, if $K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } < 0$ but $\vartheta ( s , a ) < B _ { \mathrm { a c } } ,$ raising only that logit makes $N _ { s } ^ { \star }$ strictly negative. Hence $\vartheta \in \mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$

Conversely, suppose $\vartheta \in \mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ . The endpoint signs imply, for every feasible $\eta ,$

$$
N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \eta ) - N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \vartheta ( s , \cdot ) ) = \sum _ { a } ( e ^ { \eta ( a ) } - e ^ { \vartheta ( s , a ) } ) K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } \ge 0 .
$$

The restricted Bellman minimum is zero, so every feasible numerator is nonnegative. Moreover, compactness gives a minimizer $\bar { \eta } _ { s }$ with $N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \bar { \eta } _ { s } ) = 0$ . The preceding comparison, together with the feasibility of $\vartheta ( s , \cdot )$ , therefore gives $N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \vartheta ( s , \cdot ) ) = 0$ . Thus

$$
[ T _ { \vartheta } V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ] ( s ) - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ( s ) = \frac { N _ { s } ^ { \star } ( \vartheta ( s , \cdot ) ) } { Z _ { s } ( \vartheta ( s , \cdot ) ) } = 0 .
$$

Since this holds for every state, $V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ is the fixed point of $T _ { \vartheta }$ . Uniqueness of that fixed point gives $V _ { \vartheta } = V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ , and hence

$$
J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ) = 0 \quad \Longleftrightarrow \quad \vartheta \in \mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } .\tag{S.8.7}
$$

If $\vartheta \in \mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ , the equality $V _ { \vartheta } ~ = ~ V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ gives $K _ { s , a } ( \vartheta ) = K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } .$ and the endpoint conditions give $\chi _ { s , a } ( \vartheta ) K _ { s , a } ( \vartheta ) = 0$ for every $( s , a )$ . Conversely, if these products vanish, the residual bound gives $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ) = 0 .$ so (S.8.7) yields $\vartheta \in \mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ . Since $q _ { \vartheta } ( s , a ) > 0$ , the products vanish exactly when the projected actor velocity vanishes. Thus $\mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ , equivalently the zero set of $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ , is precisely the equilibrium set of the projected actor ODE.

If $K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } = 0$ for every $( s , a )$ , then $\mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } = \Theta _ { \mathrm { a c } } , J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ is identically zero, and we set $T _ { \mathrm { h i t } } : = 0$ . The finite-hitting property is then immediate. Henceforth suppose that the nonzero restricted-gap set is nonempty, and let

$$
\Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } \neq 0 } | K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } | > 0 , \qquad \eta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } : = \frac { \Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } } { 2 ( 1 + \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } ) } .
$$

Since $V _ { \vartheta } - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ is componentwise nonnegative,

$$
\left\| V _ { \vartheta } - V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ) ,
$$

and hence

$$
| K _ { s , a } ( \vartheta ) - K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } | \leq ( 1 + \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } ) J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \vartheta ) .
$$

By (S.8.6), every ODE trajectory enters the sublevel $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } \leq \eta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ within time at most

$$
T _ { \mathrm { e n t } } : = \frac { 1 } { \kappa _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \mathrm { a c } } \eta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } } ,
$$

with entry time zero if it starts in that sublevel. This sublevel is invariant because $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ is nonincreasing. Thereafter every nonzero $K _ { s , a } ^ { \star }$ has its final sign and $| K _ { s , a } ( \vartheta ) | \geq \Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } / 2$ . Each corresponding logit therefore moves toward its required endpoint at speed at least $q _ { \mathrm { m i n } } ^ { \mathrm { a c } } \Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } / 2$ , and reaches it within an additional time at most

$$
{ \frac { 4 B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } { q _ { \mathrm { m i n } } ^ { \mathrm { a c } } \Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } } } .
$$

It remains there because the sign is preserved. In the nondegenerate case one may therefore take

$$
T _ { \mathrm { h i t } } : = T _ { \mathrm { e n t } } + \frac { 4 B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } { q _ { \mathrm { m i n } } ^ { \mathrm { a c } } \Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } } < \infty .
$$

Together with the choice $T _ { \mathrm { h i t } } : = 0$ in the degenerate case, in either case

$$
J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ( \mathsf { S } _ { t } ( \vartheta ) ) = 0 , \qquad \vartheta \in \Theta _ { \mathrm { a c } } , \quad t \geq T _ { \mathrm { h i t } } .\tag{S.8.8}
$$

For any block horizon $T \geq T _ { \mathrm { h i t } }$ and any $\rho _ { T } \in ( 0 , 1 )$ , the uniform one-block contraction condition follows.

Approximation error from the bounded-logit restriction. It remains to bound the difference between the optimal value $V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ over policies representable by logits in $[ - B _ { \mathrm { a c } } , B _ { \mathrm { a c } } ] ^ { | \mathcal { A } | }$ and the unrestricted optimal value $V ^ { \star }$ . For each state s, choose an unrestricted optimal action $a ^ { \star } ( s )$ , and let $\widehat { \vartheta } \in \Theta _ { \mathrm { a c } }$ assign logit $B _ { \mathrm { a c } }$ to $a ^ { \star } ( s )$ and logit $- B _ { \mathrm { a c } }$ to every other action. Under $\pi _ { \widehat { \vartheta } } ,$ the total probability of the other actions is at most $( | \mathcal { A } | - 1 ) e ^ { - 2 B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ . Bellman optimality and $\left\| V ^ { \star } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq C _ { \mathrm { m a x } } / ( 1 - \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } )$ give, for every $s \in { \mathcal { S } }$ and $a \in { \mathcal { A } }$

$$
0 \leq c ( s , a ) + \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } \sum _ { s ^ { \prime } \in S } P ( s ^ { \prime } \mid s , a ) V ^ { \star } ( s ^ { \prime } ) - V ^ { \star } ( s ) \leq \frac { 2 C _ { \operatorname* { m a x } } } { 1 - \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } } ,
$$

with equality on the left when $a = a ^ { \star } ( s )$ . Consequently,

$$
\left\| T _ { \widehat { \vartheta } } V ^ { \star } - V ^ { \star } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq \frac { 2 ( | A | - 1 ) C _ { \mathrm { m a x } } e ^ { - 2 B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } } { 1 - \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } } .
$$

Since $T _ { \widehat { \vartheta } }$ is a $\gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } }$ -contraction, the fixed-point residual inequality gives

$$
\left\| { V _ { \widehat { \vartheta } } - V ^ { \star } } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq \frac { 1 } { 1 - \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } } \left\| { T _ { \widehat { \vartheta } } V ^ { \star } - V ^ { \star } } \right\| _ { \infty } .
$$

Finally, optimality over the unrestricted and restricted policy classes gives $V ^ { \star } \le { \cal V } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } \le { \cal V } _ { \widehat { \vartheta } }$ componentwise, and therefore

$$
\left\| V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } - V ^ { \star } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq \frac { 2 ( | A | - 1 ) C _ { \mathrm { m a x } } e ^ { - 2 B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } } { ( 1 - \gamma _ { \mathrm { d i s c } } ) ^ { 2 } } .
$$

Summing over states proves the restriction-bias bound in the corollary.

It remains to apply Proposition 8.1. The Bellman resolvent and smoothness of the softmax map on the compact box show that $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ is Lipschitz. Taking $T = \mathrm { m a x } \{ 1 , T _ { \mathrm { h i t } } \}$ and $\rho _ { T } = e ^ { - T }$ , (S.8.8) verifies the one-block Lyapunov decrease condition needed for initial convergence. The equilibrium set $\mathcal { E } _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } }$ is a box face, and $V _ { \vartheta } = V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star }$ throughout that face. For a coordinate fixed on the face, the outward sign is $- \operatorname { s g n } ( K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } )$ , so its signed averaged drift satisfies

$$
q _ { \vartheta } ( s , a ) | K _ { s , a } ^ { \star } | \geq q _ { \mathrm { m i n } } ^ { \mathrm { a c } } \Delta _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } > 0 .
$$

If no coordinate is fixed, the outward-drift condition is vacuous and $J _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } \equiv 0$ . All other model, Lipschitz, and contraction assumptions were verified above. Both schedules in (8.9) have $0 < \alpha _ { n } \leq$ 1, so the critic cube remains invariant for either choice. Proposition 8.1, with common fast target $V _ { B _ { \mathrm { a c } } } ^ { \star } ,$ gives the component bounds and the joint rate (9.5). Together with the restriction-bias estimate already proved, this establishes Corollary 9.1. □

## S.9 Extension: Projection of the Fast-Scale Iterate

This section gives an optional extension of the framework in which the fast iterate is projected at every iteration. The main-paper recursion and the results proved for it are not changed. Instead, throughout this section we replace (1.1) by

$$
x _ { n + 1 } = \mathrm { p r o j } _ { \mathcal { X } } \left( x _ { n } + \alpha _ { n } \{ h ( Y _ { n } , x _ { n } , \theta _ { n } ) - x _ { n } + M _ { n + 1 } \} \right) , \qquad x _ { 1 } \in \mathcal { X } ,\tag{S.9.1}
$$

while retaining the projected slow recursion (1.2). Here $\mathcal { X } \subset \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } }$ is fixed: it does not depend on n or on θ. Consequently $x _ { n } \in \mathcal { X }$ for every $n ,$ and the fast-stability Assumption 3.7 is no longer needed.

This extension is useful when boundedness of the unprojected fast iterates is not available a priori or would require a separate application-specific stability proof. The main paper assumes such boundedness, whereas the modified recursion enforces it by projecting the fast iterate at every step onto a prescribed compact feasible region. Subject to the Euclidean contraction condition on X and the bounded-preprojection-noise condition stated below, the extension then gives the corresponding high-probability path-tracking and almost-sure last-iterate guarantees.

## S.9.1 Euclidean projection and contraction

Throughout this section, the fast-coordinate norm is the Euclidean norm. Let $\mathcal { X } \subset \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } }$ be a nonempty compact convex polyhedron, and write $P \chi : = \mathrm { p r o j } _ { \chi }$ for its Euclidean projection. We impose the averaged-contraction condition in the Euclidean norm and only on X:

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \theta ) - h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ^ { \prime } ; \theta ) \right\| \leq \gamma \left\| x - x ^ { \prime } \right\| , \qquad x , x ^ { \prime } \in \mathcal { X } , \quad \theta \in \Theta . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.2}
$$

Throughout this section, (S.9.2) replaces the global contraction requirement in Assumption 3.4. Assumption 3.5 is retained with both fast-state arguments restricted to X: there exist finite constants $L _ { f } ^ { \mathcal { X } } , \bar { L } _ { h , x } ^ { \mathcal { X } }$ and $L _ { h , \theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } }$ such that, uniformly over $y \in { \mathcal { V } } , x , { \tilde { x } } \in { \mathcal { X } }$ , and $\theta , { \widetilde { \theta } } \in \Theta$

$$
\left\| f ( y , x , \theta ) - f ( y , \widetilde { x } , \widetilde { \theta } ) \right\| \leq L _ { f } ^ { \chi } ( \| x - \widetilde { x } \| + \left\| \theta - \widetilde { \theta } \right\| ) ,
$$

and

$$
\left\| h ( y , x , \theta ) - h ( y , \widetilde x , \widetilde \theta ) \right\| \leq L _ { h , x } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \left\| x - \widetilde x \right\| + L _ { h , \theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \left\| \theta - \widetilde \theta \right\| .
$$

For every $v \in D _ { B V } ( [ 0 , \infty ) ; \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } } )$ with $v ( 0 ) \in \mathcal { X }$ , let $\Gamma _ { \mathcal { X } } ( v )$ denote the projected component of the Skorokhod problem on $\mathcal { X } _ { : }$ as in Definition S.13.1, with $( \Theta , d )$ replaced by $( \mathcal { X } , d _ { x } )$ . Theorem S.13.1 ensures that $\Gamma _ { \mathcal { X } }$ is well defined.

Proposition S.9.1 (Projection properties and contraction of $\mathcal { T } _ { s , \theta } )$ . Under (S.9.2), the following properties hold

(i) The Euclidean projection is nonexpansive:

$$
\| P \chi ( u ) - P \chi ( v ) \| ^ { 2 } \leq \langle P \chi ( u ) - P \chi ( v ) , u - v \rangle , \qquad u , v \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } } .
$$

Consequently, $\lVert P \chi ( u ) - P \chi ( v ) \rVert \leq \lVert u - v \rVert$

(ii) The Skorokhod map $\Gamma _ { \mathcal { X } }$ is Lipschitz: there exists $L _ { \mathrm { S k } } ^ { x } < \infty$ , independent of the time horizon, such that

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { 0 \leq t \leq H } \| \Gamma \chi ( v ) ( t ) - \Gamma \chi ( \widetilde v ) ( t ) \| \leq L _ { \mathrm { S k } } ^ { x } \operatorname* { s u p } _ { 0 \leq t \leq H } \| v ( t ) - \widetilde v ( t ) \|
$$

for every $H < \infty$ and every

$$
\begin{array} { r } { v , \widetilde { v } \in D _ { B V } ( [ 0 , \infty ) ; { \mathbb R } ^ { d _ { x } } ) \quad w i t h \quad v ( 0 ) , \widetilde { v } ( 0 ) \in \mathcal { X } . } \end{array}
$$

(iii) For $s \in [ 0 , 1 ]$ , deine

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { T } _ { s , \theta } ( x ) : = P _ { \mathcal { X } } \big ( ( 1 - s ) x + s h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \theta ) \big ) . } \end{array}
$$

Then

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \| \mathcal T _ { s , \theta } ( x ) - \mathcal T _ { s , \theta } ( x ^ { \prime } ) \| \leq \{ 1 - ( 1 - \gamma ) s \} \left\| x - x ^ { \prime } \right\| , \qquad x , x ^ { \prime } \in \mathcal X . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.3}
$$

In particular, $P _ { \mathcal { X } } \circ h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( \cdot ; \theta )$ is a γ-contraction on $\mathcal { X }$

Proof. Let $p = P _ { \mathcal { X } } ( u )$ and $q = P _ { \mathcal { X } } ( v )$ . The two projection variational inequalities give

$$
\left. u - p , q - p \right. \leq 0 , \qquad \left. v - q , p - q \right. \leq 0 .
$$

Adding them yields $\left\| p - q \right\| ^ { 2 } \leq \langle p - q , u - v \rangle$ , which proves part (i). Part (ii) follows by applying Theorem S.13.1 to the polyhedron X. Finally, part (i) and (S.9.2) give

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \left\| { \mathcal T } _ { s , \theta } ( x ) - { \mathcal T } _ { s , \theta } ( x ^ { \prime } ) \right\| \leq \left\| ( 1 - s ) ( x - x ^ { \prime } ) + s \{ h ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( x ; \theta ) - h ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( x ^ { \prime } ; \theta ) \} \right\| } \\ & { \qquad \leq \{ 1 - ( 1 - \gamma ) s \} \left\| x - x ^ { \prime } \right\| , } \end{array}
$$

which proves part (iii).

All remaining standing assumptions are imposed on $\mathcal { V } \times \mathcal { X } \times \Theta$ . In particular, we retain Assumptions 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.5, and 3.6, with the fast-coordinate bounds interpreted in the Euclidean norm; Assumption 3.4 is replaced by (S.9.2). Let $R _ { \mathcal { X } } : = \mathrm { s u p } _ { x \in \mathcal { X } } \| x \| < \infty$ . Finiteness of $\mathcal { V }$ compactness, and Assumption 3.5 give a finite $\mathsf { B } _ { \mathcal { X } }$ bounding both drift maps on $\mathcal { V } \times \mathcal { X } \times \Theta$ , while Assumption 3.3 gives the preprojection-noise bound $N _ { \mathrm { n o i s e } } ^ { \mathcal { X } } : = { \mathfrak { c } } _ { 1 } + { \mathfrak { c } } _ { 2 } R _ { \mathcal { X } }$ . Set

$$
A _ { x } ^ { \mathcal { X } } : = R _ { \mathcal { X } } + \mathsf { B } _ { \mathcal { X } } + N _ { \mathrm { n o i s e } } ^ { \mathcal { X } } , \qquad A _ { \theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } : = \mathsf { B } _ { \mathcal { X } } + N _ { \mathrm { n o i s e } } ^ { \mathcal { X } } .\tag{S.9.4}
$$

Then, almost surely,

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { } & { \underset { x \in \mathcal { X } , \theta \in \Theta , y \in \mathcal { Y } } { \operatorname* { s u p } } \{ \| x \| + \| h ( y , x , \theta ) \| \} + \underset { n } { \operatorname* { s u p } } \| M _ { n + 1 } \| \le A _ { x } ^ { \mathcal { X } } , } \\ & { } & { \| x _ { n + 1 } - x _ { n } \| \le A _ { x } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \alpha _ { n } , \qquad \| \theta _ { n + 1 } - \theta _ { n } \| \le A _ { \theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \beta _ { n } . \quad } \end{array}\tag{S.9.5}
$$

The first increment bound follows from $x _ { n } = P \chi ( x _ { n } )$ and Proposition S.9.1(i). Thus fast projection bounds the state but does not by itself bound the preprojection noise. The pathwise bounded-noise part of Assumption 3.3 is still required.

## S.9.2 Constrained equilibrium and projected fast ODE

For $x \in \mathcal { X }$ and $v \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } }$ , define the fast-set analogue of (4.1) by

$$
\Pi _ { \mathcal { X } } ( x , v ) : = \operatorname* { l i m } _ { \eta \downarrow 0 } \frac { P \chi ( x + \eta v ) - x } { \eta } .\tag{S.9.6}
$$

Let $L _ { \bar { h } , \theta }$ be a uniform Lipschitz constant of $h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \cdot )$ on Θ, uniform in $x \in \mathcal { X }$ . Such a constant follows from Assumptions 3.2, 3.5, and 3.6, exactly as in Lemma 3.1, because X is compact.

Proposition S.9.2 (Constrained fast equilibrium and exponential stability). Under the contraction condition (S.9.2), for every $\theta \in \Theta$ , the map $P \chi \circ h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( \cdot ; \theta )$ has a unique fxed point in X. Denote it by $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ ; equivalently

$$
x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) = P _ { \mathcal { X } } \Big ( h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) ; \theta ) \Big ) .\tag{S.9.7}
$$

Moreover,

$$
\| x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) \| \leq \frac { L _ { \bar { h } , \theta } } { q } \| \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \| , \qquad \theta , \theta ^ { \prime } \in \Theta .\tag{S.9.8}
$$

For frozen $\theta ,$ the projected fast ODE

$$
\dot { z } ( t ) = \Pi _ { \mathcal { X } } \big ( z ( t ) , h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ( t ) ; \theta ) - z ( t ) \big )\tag{S.9.9}
$$

has $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ as its unique equilibrium, and its flow $\mathsf { S } _ { t } ^ { x , \theta }$ satisfies

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \mathsf { S } _ { t } ^ { x , \theta } ( z ) - \mathsf { S } _ { t } ^ { x , \theta } ( z ^ { \prime } ) \right\| \le e ^ { - q t } \left\| z - z ^ { \prime } \right\| , \qquad z , z ^ { \prime } \in \mathcal { X } , \quad t \ge 0 . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.10}
$$

In particular,

$$
\left\| \mathsf { S } _ { t } ^ { x , \theta } ( z ) - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) \right\| \leq e ^ { - q t } \left\| z - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) \right\| .
$$

Proof. Proposition S.9.1(iii) and Banach's fixed-point theorem prove existence and uniqueness. Comparing the two fixed-point identities at $\theta$ and $\theta ^ { \prime }$ gives

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \| \boldsymbol { x } _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ) - \boldsymbol { x } _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ^ { \prime } ) \| \leq \gamma \| \boldsymbol { x } _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ) - \boldsymbol { x } _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ^ { \prime } ) \| + L _ { \bar { h } , \theta } \| \boldsymbol { \theta } - \boldsymbol { \theta } ^ { \prime } \| , } \end{array}
$$

which proves (S.9.8).

The projection characterization also shows that $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ is a fixed point of $\mathcal { T } _ { s , \theta }$ for every $s \in ( 0 , 1 ]$ indeed, $x = P _ { \mathcal { X } } ( h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \theta ) )$ is equivalent to $h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \stackrel { \cdot \vartriangle } { \theta } ) - x$ belonging to the normal cone at $x ,$ and that cone is positively homogeneous.

Let

$$
N _ { \mathcal { X } } ( z ) : = \left\{ \eta \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { \boldsymbol { x } } } : \langle \eta , y - z \rangle \leq 0 \mathrm { ~ f o r ~ e v e r y ~ } y \in \mathcal { X } \right\}
$$

be the Euclidean outward normal cone. For fixed $\theta ,$ the map $x \mapsto h ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( x ; \theta ) - x$ is Lipschitz on $\mathcal { X }$ with constant at most $1 + \gamma$ . Hence the projected ODE is well posed by the same projecteddynamical-systems argument as in Lemma S.15.1. The projection characterization shows that the fixed point $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ is an equilibrium of the ODE. For two solutions, the tangent-normal decomposition gives, for almost every $t ,$

$$
\dot { z } = h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ; \theta ) - z - \eta , \qquad \dot { z } ^ { \prime } = h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ^ { \prime } ; \theta ) - z ^ { \prime } - \eta ^ { \prime } ,
$$

where $\eta \in N _ { \mathcal { X } } ( z )$ and $\eta ^ { \prime } \in N _ { \mathcal { X } } ( z ^ { \prime } )$ . Here the normals are outward, $\mathrm { s o } - \eta$ and $- \eta ^ { \prime }$ are the corresponding inward correction terms. Since the normal-cone map is monotone,

$$
\langle \eta - \eta ^ { \prime } , z - z ^ { \prime } \rangle \ge 0 .
$$

Therefore,

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } {  { \frac { d } { d t } \frac { 1 } { 2 } \| z - z ^ { \prime } \| ^ { 2 } =  z - z ^ { \prime } , h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ; \theta ) - h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ^ { \prime } ; \theta )  } } \\ & { } & { \quad - \| z - z ^ { \prime } \| ^ { 2 } -  z - z ^ { \prime } , \eta - \eta ^ { \prime }  } \\ & { } & { \quad \le - ( 1 - \gamma ) \| z - z ^ { \prime } \| ^ { 2 } = - q \| z - z ^ { \prime } \| ^ { 2 } . } \end{array}
$$

Gronwall's inequality proves (S.9.10). It also proves uniqueness of the equilibrium and the final displayed bound in the proposition. □

The constrained target need not equal the fixed point used in the unprojected framework. For comparison with that framework, suppose additionally that the original global Assumption 3.4 holds, and let $x ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ denote its unique fixed point. If

$$
x ^ { \star } ( \theta ) \in \mathcal { X } , \qquad \theta \in \Theta ,\tag{S.9.11}
$$

then $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) = x ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ , so the projection is only a safeguard and the reduced slow ODE is unchanged. Without this additional global assumption and compatibility condition, the extension instead analyzes the constrained reduced field

$$
f _ { \chi } ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \theta ; \theta ^ { \prime } ) : = \sum _ { y \in \mathcal { V } } \mu ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) f \bigl ( y , x _ { \chi } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) , \theta \bigr ) ,\tag{S.9.12}
$$

and the slow projected ODE

$$
\dot { z } = \Pi _ { \Theta } \big ( z , f _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ; z ) \big ) .\tag{S.9.13}
$$

The field $\theta \mapsto f _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( \theta ; \theta )$ is Lipschitz by (S.9.8) and the standing assumptions; hence the same projected-dynamical-systems argument as in Lemma S.15.1 makes this ODE well posed. Let $\mathsf { S } _ { t } ^ { \mathcal { X } }$ denote the flow of (S.9.13), and let

$$
\mathcal { E Q } _ { \Theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } : = \left\{ z \in \Theta : \Pi _ { \Theta } ( z , f _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( z ; z ) ) = \mathbf { 0 } \right\}\tag{S.9.14}
$$

be its equilibrium set. In this section, the constrained analogues of Assumptions 8.1, 8.2, and 8.3 mean those assumptions with $\mathsf { S } _ { s } , \mathcal { E } \mathcal { Q } _ { \Theta }$ , and $f ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } }$ replaced, respectively, by $\mathsf { S } _ { s } ^ { \mathcal { X } } , \mathcal { E } \mathcal { Q } _ { \Theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } }$ , and $f _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } }$ When the constrained analogue of Assumption 8.3 is invoked, denote its one-block contraction factor by $\rho _ { T } ^ { \chi } \in ( 0 , 1 )$ . If X fails to contain $x ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ for some $\theta \in \Theta$ , the projected recursion targets $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta )$ instead, and the resulting reduced slow field, limiting ODE, and equilibrium set may differ from those of the unprojected recursion.

## S.9.3 Fixed fast-time blocks and the moving-parameter comparison

The projected-fast comparison cannot be run over an entire slow-time block by a direct Gronwall argument: the fast-time duration of such a block is of order $\alpha _ { n _ { m } } / \beta _ { n _ { m } }$ , which diverges. We therefore partition each slow block into subblocks having a fixed duration in the fast clock.

Fix a fast-time horizon $T _ { x } > 0$ . On the slow block $\kappa _ { m } .$ put $\nu _ { m , 0 } : = n _ { m }$ , and, while $\nu _ { m , j } < n _ { m + 1 }$ 2 define

$$
\nu _ { m , j + 1 } : = \operatorname* { m i n } \left\{ n _ { m + 1 } , \operatorname* { i n f } \left\{ n > \nu _ { m , j } : \sum _ { r = \nu _ { m , j } } ^ { n - 1 } \alpha _ { r } \geq T _ { x } \right\} \right\} .\tag{S.9.15}
$$

Here, m indexes the slow-time blocks and $j$ indexes the fast-time subblocks within $\kappa _ { m }$ . The index $\nu _ { m , j }$ is the left endpoint of the $j \cdot$ th subblock, while $\nu _ { m , j + 1 }$ is its right endpoint, chosen when the

accumulated fast-clock time first reaches $T _ { x } .$ or as $n _ { m + 1 }$ if the slow block ends first. Let $N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } }$ be the number of resulting subblocks. For $j = 0 , \ldots , N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } } - 1$ , define the discrete index set

$$
I _ { m , j } : = \{ r \in \mathbb { N } : \nu _ { m , j } \leq r < \nu _ { m , j + 1 } \} , \qquad H _ { m , j } ^ { x } : = \sum _ { r \in I _ { m , j } } \alpha _ { r } .
$$

Every subblock except possibly the last one satisfies

$$
T _ { x } \leq H _ { m , j } ^ { x } < T _ { x } + \alpha _ { \nu _ { m , j } } ,\tag{S.9.16}
$$

and the last subblock satisfies the corresponding upper bound. The arguments below use only the bounds in (S.9.16) on the accumulated fast time of each subblock. For integers $r < s ,$ call the index set $\{ r , r + 1 , \ldots , s - 1 \}$ admissible if

$$
\sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \le T _ { x } + \alpha _ { r } ,
$$

and call it full if the same sum is also at least $T _ { x }$ . Thus every local subblock is admissible, and every one except possibly the last is full. The terms admissible and full will also be used for the discrete index sets $\{ \nu _ { j } , \nu _ { j } + 1 , \ldots , \nu _ { j + 1 } - 1 \}$ in the trajectory-wide fast-clock partition defined later in (S.9.44). Unlike the local subblocks $I _ { m , j }$ , these blocks partition the entire iteration suffix and are not restarted or truncated at the slow-block endpoints $n _ { m + 1 }$

For each admissible block with integer endpoints $r < s .$ ,define a fresh block-restarted, movingparameter averaged recursion $\{ \widetilde { x } _ { k } \} _ { k = r } ^ { s }$ by

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \quad \widetilde { x } _ { r } : = x _ { r } , } \\ & { \widetilde { x } _ { k + 1 } : = P _ { \mathcal { X } } \left( \widetilde { x } _ { k } + \alpha _ { k } \{ h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( \widetilde { x } _ { k } ; \theta _ { k } ) - \widetilde { x } _ { k } \} \right) , \qquad r \leq k < s . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.17}
$$

The dependence of $\widetilde { x } _ { k }$ on the selected block endpoints $r , s$ is suppressed. In particular, for the local subblock $I _ { m , j }$ , we take $r = \nu _ { m , j }$ and $s = \nu _ { m , j + 1 }$ , and restart the auxiliary recursion from $x _ { \nu _ { m , j } } .$ The auxiliary recursion uses the actual moving parameter $\theta _ { k }$

We retain the fast Poisson solution from Definition 6.1, now restricted to $( x , \theta ) \in \mathcal { X } \times \Theta$ , rather than $( x , \theta ) \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } } \times \Theta$ . With $M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime }$ as in (6.4), set

$$
\Xi _ { n + 1 } : = { \cal M } _ { n + 1 } + { \cal M } _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime } , \qquad \Delta _ { n + 1 } ^ { h } : = u _ { h } ^ { ( x _ { n } , \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n } ) - u _ { h } ^ { ( x _ { n } , \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n + 1 } ) .
$$

Adding and subtracting $u _ { h } ^ { ( x _ { n } , \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n + 1 } )$ in the Poisson equation, as in Lemma 6.1, gives, pathwise,

$$
h ( Y _ { n } , x _ { n } , \theta _ { n } ) - h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x _ { n } ; \theta _ { n } ) + M _ { n + 1 } = \Xi _ { n + 1 } + \Delta _ { n + 1 } ^ { h } .\tag{S.9.18}
$$

The sequence $\{ \Xi _ { n + 1 } \}$ is a martingale-difference sequence with respect to $\{ \mathcal { F } _ { n } \}$ . Since  is finite and $\mathcal { X } \times \Theta$ is compact, the retained bounded-noise and Lipschitz assumptions, together with Assumption 3.6, give deterministic constants $C _ { \Xi } , U _ { h } ^ { \chi } , C _ { u } < \infty$ such that the first bound below holds almost surely for every $n \geq 1$ , whereas the remaining two bounds hold for every $( x , \theta ) , ( x ^ { \prime } , \theta ^ { \prime } ) \in \mathcal { X } \times \Theta$

$$
\begin{array} { c } { \displaystyle \| \Xi _ { n + 1 } \| \le C _ { \Xi } , } \\ { \displaystyle \operatorname* { s u p } _ { y \in \mathcal { V } } \left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \le U _ { h } ^ { \chi } , } \\ { \displaystyle \operatorname* { s u p } _ { y \in \mathcal { V } } \left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) - u _ { h } ^ { ( x ^ { \prime } , \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) \right\| \le C _ { u } \{ \left\| x - x ^ { \prime } \right\| + \left\| \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \right\| \} . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.19}
$$

For $r \leq k \leq s$ , define

$$
\mathcal { N } _ { r , k } ^ { x } : = \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \Xi _ { i + 1 } ,\tag{S.9.20}
$$

$$
\mathcal { R } _ { r , k } ^ { x } : = \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \Delta _ { i + 1 } ^ { h } ,\tag{S.9.21}
$$

and, for $\delta > 0$ let

$$
\mathcal { G } _ { r , s } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta ) : = \left\{ \operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \leq k \leq s } \left\| \mathcal { N } _ { r , k } ^ { x } \right\| < \delta \right\} .\tag{S.9.22}
$$

Lemma S.9.1 (Fast martingale and Poisson residual on a fast-time block). There are constants $c _ { x } , C _ { x } ^ { \mathrm { r e s } } \in ( 0 , \infty )$ , independent of $r , s ,$ such that every admissible block with endpoints $r < s$ satisies

$$
\mathbb { P } \left( [ \mathcal { G } _ { r , s } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta ) ] ^ { c } \right) \le 2 d _ { x } ( s - r ) \exp \left\{ - \frac { c _ { x } \delta ^ { 2 } } { \alpha _ { r } } \right\} ,\tag{S.9.23}
$$

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \leq k \leq s } \left\| \mathcal { R } _ { r , k } ^ { x } \right\| \leq C _ { x } ^ { \mathrm { r e s } } \alpha _ { r } .\tag{S.9.24}
$$

Proof. Coordinatewise Azuma-Hoeffding, followed by a union bound over the coordinates and the at most $s - r$ partial sums, gives

$$
\mathbb { P } \left( \operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \leq k \leq s } \left\| \mathcal { N } _ { r , k } ^ { x } \right\| \geq \delta \right) \leq 2 d _ { x } ( s - r ) \exp \left\{ - \frac { \delta ^ { 2 } } { 2 \kappa _ { d _ { x } } ^ { 2 } C _ { \Xi } ^ { 2 } \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } ^ { 2 } } \right\} .
$$

By admissibility and monotonicity of the step sizes,

$$
\sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } ^ { 2 } \leq \alpha _ { r } \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \leq ( T _ { x } + \alpha _ { 1 } ) \alpha _ { r } .
$$

This proves (S.9.23).

For the residual, abbreviate $U _ { i } ( y ) : = u _ { h } ^ { ( x _ { i } , \theta _ { i } ) } ( y )$ . Summation by parts gives, for $r < k \leq s _ { \mathrm { : } }$

$$
\begin{array} { l } { \displaystyle \mathcal { R } _ { r , k } ^ { x } = \alpha _ { r } U _ { r } ( Y _ { r } ) - \alpha _ { k - 1 } U _ { k - 1 } ( Y _ { k } ) } \\ { \displaystyle \qquad + \sum _ { i = r + 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \{ \alpha _ { i } U _ { i } ( Y _ { i } ) - \alpha _ { i - 1 } U _ { i - 1 } ( Y _ { i } ) \} . } \end{array}
$$

Using (S.9.5) and (S.9.19), its norm is at most a constant times

$$
\alpha _ { r } + \sum _ { i = r + 1 } ^ { s - 1 } | \alpha _ { i } - \alpha _ { i - 1 } | + \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } ^ { 2 } + \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \beta _ { i } .
$$

The first two terms are $O ( \alpha _ { r } )$ , because $\alpha _ { i }$ is decreasing. The third term was bounded above. Finally, $\beta _ { i } \leq \alpha _ { i }$ under Condition 3.1, so the last term is also $O ( \alpha _ { r } )$ . This proves (S.9.24). □

Lemma S.9.2 (Projected comparison on a fast-time block). There is $C _ { T _ { x } } < \infty$ , independent of $r , s _ { \mathrm { { \rangle } } }$ , such that, for every $r < s$ satisfying the admissibility condition above, on $\mathcal { G } _ { r , s } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta )$ 2

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \leq k \leq s } \| x _ { k } - \widetilde { x } _ { k } \| \leq C _ { T _ { x } } ( \delta + \alpha _ { r } ) .\tag{S.9.25}
$$

Proof. Let $F _ { x } ( x , \theta ) : = h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \theta ) - x$ On the selected block, embed the projected stochastic recursion (S.9.1) and the block-restarted averaged recursion (S.9.17) as right-continuous step paths on the local fast-time grid $\begin{array} { r } { t _ { r , k } : = \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } } \end{array}$ . Their unconstrained drivers are

$$
\begin{array} { l } { { \displaystyle v _ { r , k } : = x _ { r } + \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \{ F _ { x } ( x _ { i } , \theta _ { i } ) + \Xi _ { i + 1 } + \Delta _ { i + 1 } ^ { h } \} } , }  \\ { { \displaystyle \widetilde v _ { r , k } : = x _ { r } + \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } F _ { x } ( \widetilde x _ { i } , \theta _ { i } ) . } } \end{array}
$$

For $r \leq k < s$ and $t \in [ t _ { r , k } , t _ { r , k + 1 } )$ , set

$$
\begin{array} { r } { x ^ { \circ } ( t ) : = x _ { k } , \qquad \widetilde x ^ { \circ } ( t ) : = \widetilde x _ { k } , \qquad v ^ { \circ } ( t ) : = v _ { r , k } , \qquad \widetilde v ^ { \circ } ( t ) : = \widetilde v _ { r , k } , } \end{array}
$$

and extend all four paths constantly with their corresponding index-s values for $t \geq t _ { r , s }$ At each jump, the normal correction of the Skorokhod problem is precisely the correction made by Euclidean projection. Uniqueness of the Skorokhod problem therefore gives $x ^ { \circ } = \Gamma _ { \mathcal { X } } ( v ^ { \circ } )$ and $\widetilde x ^ { \circ } = \Gamma _ { \mathcal { X } } ( \widetilde v ^ { \circ } )$

Let $L _ { F , x }$ be the fast-state Lipschitz constant of $F _ { x }$ , and write $D _ { k } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \leq i \leq k } \| x _ { i } - \widetilde { x } _ { i } \|$ . Proposition S.9.1(ii), Lemma S.9.1, and the good event give

$$
D _ { k } \leq L _ { \mathrm { S k } } ^ { x } \left\{ L _ { F , x } \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } D _ { i } + \delta + C _ { x } ^ { \mathrm { r e s } } \alpha _ { r } \right\} .
$$

Discrete Gronwall and $\textstyle \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \leq T _ { x } + \alpha _ { 1 }$ prove (S.9.25).

For $r \leq k \leq s ,$ set

$$
\mathcal { W } _ { r , k } ^ { x } : = \prod _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } ( 1 - q \alpha _ { i } ) , \qquad \mathcal { W } _ { r , r } ^ { x } : = 1 .
$$

Lemma S.9.3 (Moving constrained equilibrium). Let $L _ { f p } ^ { \chi } : = L _ { \bar { h } , \theta } / q$ . On every admissible fast-time interval,

$$
\| \widetilde { x } _ { k } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { k } ) \| \le \mathcal { W } _ { r , k } ^ { x } \| x _ { r } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { r } ) \| + \frac { L _ { f p } ^ { \chi } A _ { \theta } ^ { \chi } } { q } \frac { \beta _ { r } } { \alpha _ { r } } , \qquad r \le k \le s .\tag{S.9.26}
$$

Proof. For $r \leq i < s .$ the auxiliary recursion (S.9.17) can be written as

$$
\widetilde { x } _ { i + 1 } = \tau _ { \alpha _ { i } , \theta _ { i } } ( \widetilde { x } _ { i } ) .
$$

As observed in the proof of Proposition S.9.2, $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } )$ is a fixed point of every relaxed map $\mathcal { T } _ { a , \theta _ { i } }$ $a \in ( 0 , 1 ]$ . Hence

$$
\mathcal { T } _ { \alpha _ { i } , \theta _ { i } } ( x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } ) ) = x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } ) .
$$

Adding and subtracting $x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } )$ , and then using the relaxed contraction (S.9.3), the equilibrium Lipschitz bound (S.9.8), and the slow-increment bound (S.9.5), gives

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \| \widetilde { x } _ { i + 1 } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i + 1 } ) \| \leq \| { \mathcal { T } } _ { \alpha _ { i } , \theta _ { i } } ( \widetilde { x } _ { i } ) - { \mathcal { T } } _ { \alpha _ { i } , \theta _ { i } } ( x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } ) ) \| + \| x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } ) - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i + 1 } ) \| } \\ & { \qquad \leq \left( 1 - q \alpha _ { i } \right) \| \widetilde { x } _ { i } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } ) \| + L _ { f p } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \| \theta _ { i + 1 } - \theta _ { i } \| } \\ & { \qquad \leq \left( 1 - q \alpha _ { i } \right) \| \widetilde { x } _ { i } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { i } ) \| + L _ { f p } ^ { \mathcal { X } } A _ { \theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \beta _ { i } . } \end{array}
$$

Iterate this inequality. Since $\beta _ { i } / \alpha _ { i } \le \beta _ { r } / \alpha _ { r }$ for $i \geq r ,$ the forcing sum is at most

$$
L _ { f p } ^ { \chi } A _ { \theta } ^ { \chi } \frac { \beta _ { r } } { \alpha _ { r } } \sum _ { i = r } ^ { k - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \prod _ { \ell = i + 1 } ^ { k - 1 } ( 1 - q \alpha \ell ) \leq \frac { L _ { f p } ^ { \chi } A _ { \theta } ^ { \chi } } { q } \frac { \beta _ { r } } { \alpha _ { r } } ,
$$

where the last sum telescopes after multiplication by $q .$

Combining the preceding two lemmas gives, on $\mathcal { G } _ { r , s } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta )$ ，

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \| x _ { k } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { k } ) \| \le \mathcal { W } _ { r , k } ^ { x } \| x _ { r } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { r } ) \| } \\ & { \qquad + C _ { T _ { x } } \left( \delta + \alpha _ { r } + \displaystyle \frac { \beta _ { r } } { \alpha _ { r } } \right) , \qquad r \le k \le s . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.27}
$$

If the interval is full, then

$$
\mathcal { W } _ { r , s } ^ { x } \le \exp \left\{ - q \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \alpha _ { i } \right\} \le e ^ { - q T _ { x } } = : \rho _ { x } < 1 .\tag{S.9.28}
$$

## S.9.4 Slow-block interface and high-probability event

For the remainder of this section, define the projected-fast tracking error by

$$
e _ { n } ^ { ( x ) } : = \| x _ { n } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { n } ) \| , \qquad n \geq 1 .
$$

Define the projected-fast event on the mth slow block by

$$
\mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta ) : = \bigcap _ { j = 0 } ^ { N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } } - 1 } \mathcal { G } _ { \nu _ { m , j } , \nu _ { m , j + 1 } } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta ) .\tag{S.9.29}
$$

Thus, $\mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta )$ is the event that, on every fast-time subblock of the mth slow block, the maximum accumulated martingale fluctuation is strictly less than $\delta ,$ with the accumulation restarted at each subblock entrance. On this event, (S.9.27) can be applied successively across all fast-time subblocks of the slow block.

Proposition S.9.3 (Projected-fast input to the slow proof). For every ixed slow-block horizon $T _ { i }$ there are constants $c _ { x , T } , C _ { x , T } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } \in \left( 0 , \infty \right)$ such that, for all $m \geq 0$ and $\delta > 0$

$$
\mathbb { P } \left( [ \mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta ) ] ^ { c } \right) \leq 2 d _ { x } K _ { m } \exp \left\{ - \frac { c _ { x , T } \delta ^ { 2 } } { \alpha _ { n _ { m } } } \right\} .\tag{S.9.30}
$$

On $\mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta )$

$$
\sum _ { r = n _ { m } } ^ { n _ { m + 1 } - 1 } \beta _ { r } \lVert x _ { r } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { r } ) \rVert \leq C _ { x , T } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } \left( \delta + \alpha _ { n _ { m } } + \frac { \beta _ { n _ { m } } } { \alpha _ { n _ { m } } } \right) .\tag{S.9.31}
$$

Proof. Applying the union bound to the subblock failure events in (S.9.29) and using (S.9.23), together with

$$
\sum _ { j = 0 } ^ { N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } } - 1 } ( \nu _ { m , j + 1 } - \nu _ { m , j } ) = K _ { m } , \qquad \alpha _ { \nu _ { m , j } } \leq \alpha _ { n _ { m } } ,
$$

gives (S.9.30), after decreasing the constant in the exponent if necessary. We next prove (S.9.31), which bounds the cumulative fast-tracking error weighted by the slow step sizes. Recall $g _ { m } ^ { ( x ) } ( \delta )$ from (6.14); its three terms dominate the corresponding terms on later subblocks. Iterating the endpoint form of (S.9.27) over the full fast subblocks and using (S.9.28) gives

$$
e _ { \nu _ { m , j } } ^ { ( x ) } \leq \rho _ { x } ^ { j } e _ { n _ { m } } ^ { ( x ) } + C _ { T _ { x } } g _ { m } ^ { ( x ) } ( \delta ) ,
$$

up to a change in $C _ { T _ { x } }$ . Since $x _ { n _ { m } } , x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { n _ { m } } ) \in \mathcal { X }$ , compactness of $\mathcal { X }$ gives a deterministic bound on $e _ { n _ { m } } ^ { ( x ) }$

On an admissible subblock with integer endpoints $r \ < \ s .$ , monotonicity of $\beta _ { i } / \alpha _ { i }$ and the telescoping identity used in the proof of Lemma S.9.3 yield

$$
\sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \beta _ { i } \mathcal { W } _ { r , i } ^ { x } \le \frac { \beta _ { r } } { q \alpha _ { r } } .
$$

Also,

$$
\sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \beta _ { i } \le \frac { \beta _ { r } } { \alpha _ { r } } H _ { m , j } ^ { x } \le C _ { T _ { x } } \frac { \beta _ { r } } { \alpha _ { r } } .
$$

Apply these bounds to (S.9.27) and sum over j. By the preceding entrance-error bound, the part of $e _ { \nu _ { m , j } } ^ { ( x ) }$ inherited from the initial slow-block error is bounded by $\rho _ { x } ^ { j } e _ { n _ { m } } ^ { ( x ) }$ , and hence decays geometrically across the fast subblocks. The remaining terms are controlled using

$$
\sum _ { j = 0 } ^ { N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } } - 1 } \frac { \beta _ { \nu _ { m , j } } } { \alpha _ { \nu _ { m , j } } } \leq C _ { T }
$$

because each full subblock has fast duration at least $T _ { x }$ and the total slow duration is at most $T + \beta _ { n _ { m } }$ . We verify this bound explicitly. Fixed fast duration and the power-step formulas make $( N _ { 0 } + i ) / ( N _ { 0 } + r )$ uniformly bounded on each admissible block with endpoints $r < s .$ SO $\beta _ { i } / \alpha _ { i }$ and $\beta _ { r } / \alpha _ { r }$ are uniformly comparable there. Consequently, on each full subblock,

$$
\beta _ { r } / \alpha _ { r } \leq \frac { C } { T _ { x } } \sum _ { i = r } ^ { s - 1 } \beta _ { i } .
$$

Summing over the full subblocks gives a constant times the slow duration; for the possibly short final subblock, monotonicity gives $\beta _ { r } / \alpha _ { r } \leq \beta _ { n _ { m } } / \alpha _ { n _ { m } }$ . Moreover, $\begin{array} { r } { \sum _ { j = 0 } ^ { N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } } - 1 } { \rho _ { x } ^ { j } \frac { \beta _ { \nu _ { m , j } } } { \alpha _ { \nu _ { m , j } } } } \leq \frac { \bar { \beta } _ { n _ { m } } } { \alpha _ { n _ { m } } ( 1 - \rho _ { x } ) } } \end{array}$ Thus the initial transient is multiplied by $\beta _ { n _ { m } } / \alpha _ { n _ { m } }$ , already a term in $g _ { m } ^ { ( x ) } ( \delta )$ , and no factor $N _ { m } ^ { \mathrm { s u b } }$ is lost. This proves (S.9.31). □

Equation (S.9.31) is the constrained analogue of (7.14), which controls the weighted fasttracking term in the original-auxiliary comparison (7.12). After establishing the constrained slow Poisson and martingale bounds below, the proofs of Propositions 7.1 and 7.2 carry over under the following substitutions:

$$
\begin{array} { r } { x ^ { \star } ( \theta ) \longmapsto x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) , \qquad f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } \longmapsto f _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } , \qquad \mathcal { G } _ { x } ^ { ( m ) } ( \delta ) \longmapsto \mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta ) . } \end{array}\tag{S.9.32}
$$

To make the slow-coordinate transfer explicit, define the function describing the slow Markov noise

$$
g _ { f , \chi } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) : = f \big ( y , x _ { \chi } ^ { \star } ( \theta ) , \theta \big ) - f _ { \chi } ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \theta ; \theta ) , \qquad y \in \mathcal { V } ,\tag{S.9.33}
$$

and its return-time Poisson solution

$$
u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) : = \mathbb { E } ^ { ( \theta ) } \left[ \sum _ { r = 0 } ^ { \tau - 1 } g _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( Y _ { r } ) \bigg | Y _ { 0 } = y \right] ,\tag{S.9.34}
$$

where $\tau$ is the return time used in Definition 7.1. For $n \geq 1$ , set

$$
M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime \prime , \mathcal { X } } : = u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n + 1 } ) - \sum _ { y \in \mathcal { Y } } p ^ { ( \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n } , y ) u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta _ { n } ) } ( y ) .\tag{S.9.35}
$$

Then $\{ M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime \prime , \mathcal { X } } \}$ is a martingale-difference sequence and

$$
g _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n } ) = u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n } ) - u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta _ { n } ) } ( Y _ { n + 1 } ) + M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime \prime , \mathcal { X } } .\tag{S.9.36}
$$

By compactness of X and Θ, (S.9.8), and the same killed-resolvent argument used for Lemma S.11.1, there are finite deterministic constants $U _ { f , \mathcal { X } } , C _ { f , \mathcal { X } }$ , and $C _ { \mathrm { m g } } ^ { ( \theta , { \chi } ) }$ such that, for every $\theta , \theta ^ { \prime } \in \Theta$

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { \vartheta \in \Theta , y \in \mathcal { Y } } \left. u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \vartheta ) } ( y ) \right. \leq U _ { f , \mathcal { X } } , \qquad \operatorname* { s u p } _ { y \in \mathcal { Y } } \left. u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) - u _ { f , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) \right. \leq C _ { f , \mathcal { X } } \left. \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \right. ,\tag{S.9.37}
$$

and, for every $n \geq 1 , \left. M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime } + M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime \prime , \mathcal { X } } \right. \leq C _ { \mathrm { m g } } ^ { ( \theta , \mathcal { X } ) }$ almost surely. Define

$$
\mathcal { G } _ { \theta , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( m ) } ( \delta _ { s } ) : = \left\{ \operatorname* { m a x } _ { 1 \leq k \leq K _ { m } } \left\| \sum _ { \ell = 0 } ^ { k - 1 } \beta _ { n _ { m } + \ell } ( M _ { n _ { m } + \ell + 1 } ^ { \prime } + M _ { n _ { m } + \ell + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime \prime , \mathcal { X } } ) \right\| < \delta _ { s } \right\} .\tag{S.9.38}
$$

The coordinatewise Azuma-Hoeffding argument gives

$$
\mathbb { P } \left( [ \mathcal { G } _ { \theta , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( m ) } ( \delta _ { s } ) ] ^ { c } \right) \le 2 d _ { \theta } \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K _ { m } } \exp \left\{ - \frac { \delta _ { s } ^ { 2 } } { 2 \kappa _ { d _ { \theta } } ^ { 2 } ( C _ { \mathrm { m g } } ^ { ( \theta , \mathcal { X } ) } ) ^ { 2 } \{ b ( n _ { m } ) - b ( n _ { m } + k ) \} } \right\} .\tag{S.9.39}
$$

Proposition S.9.4 (Localized slow-ODE tracking with both projections). Fix a slow-block horizon T. There is $C _ { B , T } ^ { \mathcal { X } } < \infty$ such that, for every $m \geq 0$ and $\delta _ { f } , \delta _ { s } > 0$ , on

$$
\mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta _ { f } ) \cap \mathcal { G } _ { \theta , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( m ) } ( \delta _ { s } ) ,
$$

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { 0 \leq t \leq H _ { m } } \left\| \theta ^ { ( m ) , \circ } ( t ) - z _ { \chi } ^ { ( T _ { m } ) } ( t ) \right\| \leq C _ { B , T } ^ { \chi } \left( \delta _ { f } + \delta _ { s } + \alpha _ { n _ { m } } + \frac { \beta _ { n _ { m } } } { \alpha _ { n _ { m } } } \right) ,\tag{S.9.40}
$$

where $z _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( T _ { m } ) }$ solves (S.9.13) from $\theta _ { n _ { m } }$ . Moreover, the failure probability of this event is at most the sum of the right-hand sides of (S.9.30) and (S.9.39).

Proof. Use (S.9.36) in the original slow-driver decomposition. Bounds (S.9.8) and (S.9.37) give the same Poisson residual, auxiliary-recursion, and Skorokhod estimates as in Section 7, with $x ^ { \star }$ $f ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } }$ , and the stepsize-weighted fast input replaced according to (S.9.32). Proposition S.9.3 supplies that stepsize-weighted input. The same summation-by-parts calculation gives a deterministic constrained slow-Poisson residual bounded by $C \{ \beta _ { n _ { m } } + b ( n _ { m } ) - b ( n _ { m + 1 } ) \} = O ( \beta _ { n _ { m } } )$ . The union bound gives the stated failure probability. □

For a rate-specific choice, set

$$
L _ { m } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } : = \log \{ 4 d _ { x } K _ { m } ( m + 2 ) ^ { 2 } \} , \qquad \delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( x , \mathrm { p r o j } ) } ( m ) : = \left( \frac { \alpha _ { n _ { m } } L _ { m } ^ { x , \mathrm { p r o j } } } { c _ { x , T } } \right) ^ { 1 / 2 } .\tag{S.9.41}
$$

Then (S.9.30) gives

$$
\mathbb { P } \left( [ \mathcal { H } _ { x , m } ^ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( \delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( x , \mathrm { p r o j } ) } ( m ) ) ] ^ { c } \right) \leq \frac { 1 } { 2 ( m + 2 ) ^ { 2 } } .
$$

Combine this event with $\mathcal { G } _ { \theta , \mathcal { X } } ^ { ( m ) } ( \delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( \theta , \mathcal { X } ) } ( m ) )$ , where

$$
\delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( \theta , \mathcal { X } ) } ( m ) : = \kappa _ { d _ { \theta } } C _ { \mathrm { m g } } ^ { ( \theta , \mathcal { X } ) } \sqrt { 2 D _ { m } L _ { m } ^ { \theta } } .
$$

Equations (S.9.39) and (S.9.30), followed by Borel-Cantelli, give the same eventual pathwise slowblock event as in Section 8, now with

$$
d _ { m , \mathcal { X } } ^ { \mathrm { r a t e } } : = \delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( x , \mathrm { p r o j } ) } ( m ) + \delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( \theta , \mathcal { X } ) } ( m ) + \alpha _ { n _ { m } } + \frac { \beta _ { n _ { m } } } { \alpha _ { n _ { m } } } .\tag{S.9.42}
$$

On the resulting event, Proposition S.9.4 gives

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { 0 \leq t \leq H _ { m } } \left\| \theta ^ { ( m ) , \circ } ( t ) - z _ { \chi } ^ { ( T _ { m } ) } ( t ) \right\| \leq C _ { B , T } ^ { \chi } d _ { m , \chi } ^ { \operatorname { r a t e } } ,\tag{S.9.43}
$$

## S.9.5 Almost-sure rates, including the harmonic slow step

The preceding local construction is enough for the slow proof. For completeness, a global partition of the fast clock gives the iteration-wise fast rate. Under the shifted-step condition (3.3), put $\nu _ { 0 } : = 1 ;$ under the finite-prefix alternative, put $\nu _ { 0 } : = n _ { 0 }$ , where $n _ { 0 }$ is the suffix index defined in Appendix S.16. Define

$$
\nu _ { j + 1 } : = \operatorname* { i n f } \left\{ n > \nu _ { j } : \sum _ { r = \nu _ { j } } ^ { n - 1 } \alpha _ { r } \geq T _ { x } \right\} .\tag{S.9.44}
$$

Apply (S.9.27) on these successive blocks with

$$
\delta _ { j } = C _ { \delta } \sqrt { \alpha _ { \nu _ { j } } \log ( N _ { 0 } + \nu _ { j } ) } ,
$$

where $C _ { \delta }$ is chosen so that $c _ { x } C _ { \delta } ^ { 2 } > 1$ . Since $N _ { 0 } + \nu _ { j } \asymp ( j + 1 ) ^ { 1 / ( 1 - \mathfrak { a } ) }$ , integral comparison for the power steps gives $\nu _ { j + 1 } - \nu _ { j } = O ( ( N _ { 0 } + \nu _ { j } ) ^ { \mathfrak { a } } )$ . Hence the right-hand side of (S.9.23) is at most

$$
C ( N _ { 0 } + \nu _ { j } ) ^ { { \mathfrak { a } } - c _ { x } C _ { \delta } ^ { 2 } } ,
$$

which is summable in $j .$ Therefore, eventually almost surely,

$$
e _ { \nu _ { j + 1 } } ^ { ( x ) } \leq \rho _ { x } e _ { \nu _ { j } } ^ { ( x ) } + C _ { T _ { x } } \left\{ \sqrt { \alpha _ { \nu _ { j } } \log ( N _ { 0 } + \nu _ { j } ) } + \alpha _ { \nu _ { j } } + \frac { \beta _ { \nu _ { j } } } { \alpha _ { \nu _ { j } } } \right\} .\tag{S.9.45}
$$

A geometric convolution of this recursion has the same order as its right-hand envelope. On a global fast block, $( N _ { 0 } + n ) / ( N _ { 0 } + \nu _ { j } )$ is uniformly bounded and tends to one; applying the admissibleinterval version of (S.9.27) to the final partial block therefore gives

$$
\| x _ { n } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { n } ) \| = O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \left( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , \mathfrak { b } ) } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) } \right) .\tag{S.9.46}
$$

The next theorem records the complete transfer to the slow results.

Theorem S.9.1 (Rates with projection on both time scales). Let X be a nonempty compact convex polyhedron and equip the fast coordinate with the Euclidean norm. Suppose Condition 3.1 and the standing controlled-Markov, Euclidean contraction, Lipschitz, bounded-noise, and hittingtime assumptions hold on $\mathcal { V } \times \mathcal { X } \times \Theta$ . Use either the shifted-step condition (3.3) or the finite-prefix alternative of Appendix S.16. Let J be a Lipschitz Lyapunov function satisfying the constrained analogue of Assumption 8.1 for (S.9.13). Assumption 3.7 is not required. Then (S.9.46) holds. The following slow conclusions also hold.

(i) Under the constrained analogue of the general one-block decrease condition in Assumption 8.2,

$$
J ( \theta _ { n } ) \longrightarrow 0 , \qquad \mathrm { d i s t } \bigl ( \theta _ { n } , \mathcal { E } \mathcal { Q } _ { \Theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } \bigr ) \longrightarrow 0 \quad a l m o s t \ s u r e l y ,
$$

where $\mathcal { E Q } _ { \Theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } }$ is defined in (S.9.14).

(ii) Under the hypotheses of part (i), suppose additionally that there exist $p _ { J } > 1 , \kappa _ { J } > 0$ , and $u _ { J } > 0$ such that

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { D } _ { J } ( u ) \ge \kappa _ { J } u ^ { p _ { J } } , \qquad 0 \le u \le u _ { J } . } \end{array}
$$

$I f \ 6 < 1$ , then

$$
J ( \theta _ { n } ) = O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \Big ( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - ( 1 - 6 ) / ( p _ { J } - 1 ) } + ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - r _ { x } ( \mathbf { a } , \mathbf { b } ) / p _ { J } } \{ \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) \} ^ { 1 / ( 2 p _ { J } ) } \Big ) .\tag{S.9.47}
$$

$I f { \boldsymbol { \mathfrak { b } } } = 1$ , then

$$
J ( \theta _ { n } ) = O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \Big ( \{ \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) \} ^ { - 1 / ( p _ { J } - 1 ) } \Big ) .\tag{S.9.48}
$$

Thus the harmonic rate is $O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } ( 1 / \log n )$ when $p _ { J } = 2$

(iii) Under the constrained analogue of the linear one-block contraction condition in Assumption 8.3, $i f [ \mathfrak { b } < 1 $ , then

$$
J ( \theta _ { n } ) = O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \left( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , \mathfrak { b } ) } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) } \right) .\tag{S.9.49}
$$

If b = 1, put

$$
\lambda _ { T } ^ { \chi } : = - \frac { \log \rho _ { T } ^ { \chi } } { T } > 0 .
$$

Then

$$
J ( \theta _ { n } ) = \left\{ \begin{array} { l l } { O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \Big ( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , 1 ) } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) } \Big ) , } & { \lambda _ { T } ^ { \mathcal { X } } > r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , 1 ) , } \\ { O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \Big ( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , 1 ) } \{ \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) \} ^ { 3 / 2 } \Big ) , } & { \lambda _ { T } ^ { \mathcal { X } } = r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , 1 ) , } \\ { O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \Big ( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - \lambda _ { T } ^ { \mathcal { X } } } \Big ) , } & { 0 < \lambda _ { T } ^ { \mathcal { X } } < r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , 1 ) . } \end{array} \right.\tag{S.9.50}
$$

At b = 1 and ${ \mathfrak { a } } = 2 / 3$ ，

$$
\| x _ { n } - x _ { \mathcal { X } } ^ { \star } ( \theta _ { n } ) \| = O _ { \mathrm { a . s . } } \left( ( N _ { 0 } + n ) ^ { - 1 / 3 } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n ) } \right) ,\tag{S.9.51}
$$

and the slow rate is given by (S.9.50) with $r _ { x } ( 2 / 3 , 1 ) = 1 / 3$

If, in addition, the original global Assumption 3.4 holds and (S.9.11) holds, then $\mathcal { E Q } _ { \Theta } ^ { \mathcal { X } } = \mathcal { E Q } _ { \Theta }$ , and every target and reduced ODE in the theorem is the same as in the main paper. The shifted-stepsize and inite-prefx alternatives of Appendix S.16 carry over without change: the global fast-time construction begins at 1 in the former case and at $n _ { 0 }$ in the latter.

Proof. By the block-geometry lemmas, (S.9.41) and the constrained slow threshold satisfy

$$
\delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( x , \mathrm { p r o j } ) } ( m ) = { \cal O } \left( ( N _ { 0 } + n _ { m } ) ^ { - \mathfrak { a } / 2 } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n _ { m } ) } \right) ,
$$

and

$$
\delta _ { \mathrm { d e v } } ^ { ( \theta , { \mathcal X } ) } ( m ) = O \left( ( N _ { 0 } + n _ { m } ) ^ { - { 6 / 2 } } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n _ { m } ) } \right) .
$$

Therefore

$$
d _ { m , \mathcal { X } } ^ { \mathrm { r a t e } } = O \left( ( N _ { 0 } + n _ { m } ) ^ { - r _ { x } ( \mathfrak { a } , \mathfrak { b } ) } \sqrt { \log ( N _ { 0 } + n _ { m } ) } \right) .
$$

Propositions S.9.3 and S.9.4 provide, respectively, the stepsize-weighted fast input and the localized slow-ODE tracking estimate used in the block-endpoint proofs. Those deterministic proofs use only these estimates, the Lipschitz property of J, and the stated Lyapunov decrease or contraction of the relevant flow. Repeating them with the constrained analogues defined above gives all the stated slow conclusions, including the harmonic branch. Finally, (S.9.46) follows from (S.9.45); setting $( \mathfrak { a } , \mathfrak { b } ) = ( 2 / 3 , 1 )$ gives (S.9.51). □

## S.10 Auxiliary Proofs Used for Main-Paper Section 6

Lemma S.10.1. The following bounds hold.

(i) For every $k \geq 1$

$$
\| x _ { k + 1 } - x _ { k } \| \leq A _ { x } \alpha _ { k } ,
$$

and

$$
\lVert { \boldsymbol { \theta } } _ { k + 1 } - { \boldsymbol { \theta } } _ { k } \rVert \leq A _ { \theta } \beta _ { k } .
$$

(ii) The solution (6.3) of the fast Poisson equation satisfies

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } , \qquad x \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } } , \ \theta \in \Theta , \ y \in \mathcal { V } , } \end{array}
$$

whenever $\lVert x \rVert \leq { \mathfrak { c } } _ { 6 }$

(iii) The martingale difference sequence deined in (6.4) satisfies

$$
\left\| M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime } \right\| \leq 4 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } , \qquad n \geq 1 .
$$

(iv) Define

$$
C _ { h , \mathrm { P o i s . } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } \left\{ 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } L _ { h , x } , \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \left[ 2 L _ { h , \theta } + \left( L _ { \mu } + 2 L _ { p } \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \right) \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } \right] \right\} .
$$

For all $x , x ^ { \prime } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { x } }$ with $\| x \| , \| x ^ { \prime } \| \leq \mathfrak { c } _ { 6 }$ , all $\theta , \theta ^ { \prime } \in \Theta$ , and all $y \in \mathcal { V }$

$$
\left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) - u _ { h } ^ { ( x ^ { \prime } , \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) \right\| \le C _ { h , \mathrm { P o i s . } } \left( \| x - x ^ { \prime } \| + \| \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \| \right) .
$$

Proof. For part (i), the update equations, Assumptions 3.3 and 3.7, and the definition of ${ \mathsf { B } } _ { 6 }$ give

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \| x _ { k + 1 } - x _ { k } \| \leq A _ { x } \alpha _ { k } , } \\ { \| \theta _ { k + 1 } - \theta _ { k } \| \leq A _ { \theta } \beta _ { k } , } \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality also uses nonexpansiveness of projection.

For parts (ii)−(iv), write

$$
g _ { x , \theta } ( y ) : = h ( y , x , \theta ) - h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \theta ) .
$$

When $\| x \| \le \mathfrak { c } _ { 6 } , \operatorname* { s u p } _ { y } \| g _ { x , \theta } ( y ) \| \le 2 \mathsf { B } _ { 6 }$ . The return-time representation (6.3) and Assumption 3.6 therefore imply

$$
\left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } ,
$$

proving part (ii). Part (iii) follows from (6.4), since

$$
\left\| M _ { n + 1 } ^ { \prime \prime } \right\| \leq 2 \operatorname* { s u p } _ { y } \left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq 4 { \mathfrak c } _ { 5 } { \mathsf B } _ { 6 } .
$$

It remains to prove part $( \mathrm { i v } )$ . Let $\mathcal { V } _ { 0 } : = \mathcal { V } \setminus \{ \boldsymbol { y } ^ { \star } \}$ . The claim is immediate if $\mathcal { V } _ { 0 }$ is empty. For vector-valued functions on $\mathcal { V } _ { 0 }$ , use $\| v \| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { y } \| v ( y ) \|$ ; then

$$
\left\| A v \right\| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } } \leq \left\| A \right\| _ { \infty } \left\| v \right\| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } }
$$

for every scalar matrix $A ,$ with no dimension-dependent factor.

Let $Q ^ { ( \theta ) }$ be the restriction of $p ^ { ( \theta ) }$ to $\mathcal { D } _ { 0 } \times \mathcal { D } _ { 0 }$ . The hitting-time identity gives

$$
\begin{array} { c } { { \displaystyle Z ^ { ( \theta ) } : = \sum _ { n \geq 0 } ( Q ^ { ( \theta ) } ) ^ { n } = ( I - Q ^ { ( \theta ) } ) ^ { - 1 } , } } \\ { { \displaystyle \operatorname* { s u p } _ { \theta \in \Theta } \left\| Z ^ { ( \theta ) } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } . } } \end{array}\tag{S.10.1}
$$

Assumption 3.6 makes $y ^ { \star }$ positive recurrent and gives $\mu ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ^ { \star } ) > 0$ Since $\begin{array} { r } { \sum _ { y } \mu ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) g _ { x , \theta } ( y ) = 0 } \end{array}$ 2 Lemma S.15.7, applied coordinatewise to the return-time representation (6.3), gives $u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ^ { \star } ) = 0$ First-step decomposition then yields

$$
u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } = Z ^ { ( \theta ) } g _ { x , \theta } \quad \mathrm { o n } \ y _ { 0 } .\tag{S.10.2}
$$

Set $\Delta _ { x } : = \| x - x ^ { \prime } \|$ and $\Delta _ { \theta } : = \lVert \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \rVert$ . Assumption 3.5, the bound ${ \sf B } _ { 6 }$ , and Lemma 3.1 give

$$
\left\| h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ; \theta ) - h ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( x ^ { \prime } ; \theta ^ { \prime } ) \right\| \leq L _ { h , x } \Delta _ { x } + ( L _ { h , \theta } + L _ { \mu } \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } ) \Delta _ { \theta } ,
$$

and hence

$$
\big \| g _ { x , \theta } - g _ { x ^ { \prime } , \theta ^ { \prime } } \big \| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } } \leq 2 L _ { h , x } \Delta _ { x } + ( 2 L _ { h , \theta } + L _ { \mu } \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } ) \Delta _ { \theta } .\tag{S.10.3}
$$

Also, $\| g _ { x ^ { \prime } , \theta ^ { \prime } } \| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } } \leq 2 \mathsf { B } _ { 6 }$ . Since

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left. Q ^ { ( \theta ) } - Q ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } \right. _ { \infty } \leq L _ { p } \Delta _ { \theta } , } \end{array}
$$

the resolvent identity and (S.10.1) yield

$$
\left\| Z ^ { ( \theta ) } - Z ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } ^ { 2 } L _ { p } \Delta _ { \theta } .
$$

Using (S.10.2)–(S.10.3),

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \underset { y \in \mathcal { V } _ { 0 } } { \operatorname* { s u p } } \left\| u _ { h } ^ { ( x , \theta ) } ( y ) - u _ { h } ^ { ( x ^ { \prime } , \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } L _ { h , x } \Delta _ { x } } \\ & { \qquad + \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \left[ 2 L _ { h , \theta } + ( L _ { \mu } + 2 L _ { p } \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } ) \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } \right] \Delta _ { \theta } . } \end{array}
$$

Both solutions vanish at $y ^ { \star }$ , so this bound holds on all of . Taking the larger coefficient gives $C _ { h , \mathrm { P o i s . } }$ , proving part (iv). □

## S.11 Auxiliary Results Used for Main-Paper Section 7

Lemma S.11.1. Consider the Poisson equation (7.6). The following bounds hold.

(i) For every $\theta \in \Theta$ and $y \in \mathcal { V }$

$$
\left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } .
$$

(ii) For every $\theta , \theta ^ { \prime } \in \Theta$ and $y \in \mathcal { V }$

$$
\left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq C _ { f , \mathrm { P o i s . } } \left\| \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \right\| ,
$$

where

$$
C _ { f , \mathrm { P o i s . } } : = \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \left[ 2 L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) + ( L _ { \mu } + 2 L _ { p } \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } ) \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } \right] .
$$

(iii) The martingale difference sequence deined in (7.8) satisfies

$$
\left\| ( M ^ { \prime \prime \prime } ) _ { n + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } \right\| \leq 4 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } .
$$

Proof. Set

$$
\begin{array} { r } { g _ { \boldsymbol { \theta } } ( \boldsymbol { y } ) : = f ( \boldsymbol { y } , \boldsymbol { x } ^ { \star } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ) , \boldsymbol { \theta } ) - f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ; \boldsymbol { \theta } ) . } \end{array}
$$

The definition of ${ \sf B } _ { 8 }$ gives $\mathrm { s u p } _ { y } \left\| g _ { \theta } ( y ) \right\| \leq 2 \mathsf { B } _ { 8 }$ . Hence the return-time representation and Assumption 3.6 imply

$$
\left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } ,
$$

which proves part (i).

For part (ii), let $\Delta _ { \theta } : = \lVert \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \rVert$ . Assumption 3.5 and Lemma 3.1 give

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { y } \| f ( y , x ^ { \star } ( \theta ) , \theta ) - f ( y , x ^ { \star } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) , \theta ^ { \prime } ) \| \leq L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) \Delta _ { \theta } .
$$

Adding and subtracting the stationary average with measure $\mu ^ { ( \theta ) }$ gives

$$
\left\| f ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( \theta ; \theta ) - f ^ { \mathrm { ( a v . ) } } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ; \theta ^ { \prime } ) \right\| \leq \left[ L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) + L _ { \mu } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } \right] \Delta _ { \theta } .
$$

Use the vector-valued sup norm introduced in the proof of Lemma S.10.1. The bounded-set estimate above gives

$$
\| g _ { \theta ^ { \prime } } \| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } } \leq 2 \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } .\tag{S.11.1}
$$

Combining the preceding two Lipschitz bounds gives

$$
\left\| g _ { \theta } - g _ { \theta ^ { \prime } } \right\| _ { \infty , \mathrm { v e c } } \leq [ 2 L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) + L _ { \mu } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } ] \Delta _ { \theta } .\tag{S.11.2}
$$

These are the only bounds on this function needed.

If $\mathcal { V } _ { 0 } : = \mathcal { V } \backslash \left\{ y ^ { \star } \right\}$ is empty, the claim is immediate. Otherwise, use the killed kernels $Q ^ { ( \theta ) }$ and resolvents $Z ^ { ( \theta ) }$ constructed in the proof of Lemma S.10.1. Assumption 3.6 and Lemma S.15.7, applied coordinatewise to the return-time representation (7.7), give ${ u } _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ^ { \star } ) = 0 ;$ first-step decomposition then gives

$$
u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } = Z ^ { ( \theta ) } g _ { \theta } \quad \mathrm { o n } ~ \mathcal { V } _ { 0 } , \qquad \left. Z ^ { ( \theta ) } \right. _ { \infty } \leq \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } .
$$

As in that proof,

$$
\left\| Z ^ { ( \theta ) } - Z ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } \right\| _ { \infty } \leq \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } ^ { 2 } L _ { p } \Delta _ { \theta } .
$$

Therefore, adding and subtracting $Z ^ { ( \theta ) } g _ { \theta ^ { \prime } }$ and using (S.11.1)–(S.11.2),

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { y \in \mathcal { Y } } \left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) } ( y ) \right\| \leq \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \Big [ 2 L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) + ( L _ { \mu } + 2 L _ { p } \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } ) \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } \Big ] \Delta _ { \theta } .
$$

The coefficient is $C _ { f , \mathrm { P o i s . } }$ , proving part (ii). Finally, (7.8) and part (i) give

$$
\left\| ( M ^ { \prime \prime \prime } ) _ { n + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } \right\| \le 2 \operatorname* { s u p } _ { y } \left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) \right\| \le 4 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } ,
$$

which proves part (iii).

Lemma S.11.2. For all $\theta , \theta ^ { \prime } , \tilde { \theta } \in \Theta$ , the following hold:

(i)

$$
\left\| f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ; \theta ) - f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \tilde { \theta } ; \theta ) \right\| \leq L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) \left\| \theta ^ { \prime } - \tilde { \theta } \right\| .
$$

(ii)

$$
\left\| f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \theta ; \theta ) - f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ; \theta ^ { \prime } ) \right\| \leq L _ { f , \mathrm { a v g . } } \left\| \theta - \theta ^ { \prime } \right\| ,
$$

where

$$
L _ { f , \mathrm { a v g . } } : = L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) + L _ { \mu } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } .
$$

(iii)

$$
\left\| f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ; \boldsymbol { \theta } ^ { \prime } ) - f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ; \tilde { \boldsymbol { \theta } } ) \right\| \leq L _ { \mu } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } \left\| \boldsymbol { \theta } ^ { \prime } - \tilde { \boldsymbol { \theta } } \right\| .
$$

Proof. For part (i),

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \Big \| f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ; \theta ) - f ^ { ( \mathrm { a v . } ) } ( \tilde { \theta } ; \theta ) \Big \| \leq \displaystyle \sum _ { y \in \mathcal { Y } } \mu ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) \left\| f ( y , x ^ { \star } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) , \theta ^ { \prime } ) - f ( y , x ^ { \star } ( \tilde { \theta } ) , \tilde { \theta } ) \right\| } & { } \\ { \leq L _ { f } \left( \Big \| x ^ { \star } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) - x ^ { \star } ( \tilde { \theta } ) \Big \| + \Big \| \theta ^ { \prime } - \tilde { \theta } \Big \| \right) } & { } \\ { \leq L _ { f } ( L _ { f p } + 1 ) \left\| \theta ^ { \prime } - \tilde { \theta } \right\| . } \end{array}
$$

For part (ii), add and subtract $\begin{array} { r l } { \sum _ { y \in \mathcal { V } } \mu ^ { ( \theta ) } ( y ) f ( y , x ^ { \star } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) , \theta ^ { \prime } ) } \end{array}$ and use part (i), Lemma 3.1, and the bounded-set estimate

$$
\left\| f ( y , x ^ { \star } ( \theta ^ { \prime } ) , \theta ^ { \prime } ) \right\| \leq \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } .
$$

Part (iii) follows directly from Lemma 3.1 and the same bounded-set estimate.

Lemma S.11.3 (Residual term in the Poisson decomposition). For every $1 \leq n \leq K _ { m }$

$$
\left\| \sum _ { k = 0 } ^ { n - 1 } \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } \left( u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) \right) \right\| \leq R _ { m } ,
$$

where

$$
R _ { m } = 3 C _ { P } \beta _ { n _ { m } } + C _ { L } ( b ( n _ { m } ) - b ( n _ { m + 1 } ) ) ,\tag{S.11.3}
$$

with

$$
C _ { P } : = 2 \mathfrak { c } _ { 5 } \mathsf { B } _ { 8 } , \qquad C _ { L } : = C _ { f , \mathrm { P o i s . } } ( \mathfrak { c } _ { 1 } + \mathfrak { c } _ { 2 } \mathfrak { c } _ { 6 } + \mathsf { B } _ { 6 } ) = C _ { f , \mathrm { P o i s . } } A _ { \theta } .
$$

Here $C _ { f , \mathrm { P o i s } }$ is the constant dened in part (ii) of Lemma S.11.1.

Proof of Lemma S.11.3. The sum can be written as follows:

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \displaystyle \sum _ { k = 0 } ^ { n - 1 } \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } \left( u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) \right) } \\ & { \quad = \beta _ { 0 } ^ { ( m ) } u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { 0 } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { 0 } ^ { ( m ) } ) - \beta _ { n - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { n - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { n } ^ { ( m ) } ) } \\ & { \quad \quad + \displaystyle \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { n - 1 } \left( \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - \beta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) \right) . } \end{array}
$$

The two boundary terms are bounded by

$$
\beta _ { 0 } ^ { ( m ) } C _ { P } + \beta _ { n - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } C _ { P } \leq 2 C _ { P } \beta _ { n _ { m } } .
$$

For the summation term, write

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - \beta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } \\ & { \quad = \beta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } \{ u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) \} } \\ & { \quad \quad + ( \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } - \beta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) . } \end{array}
$$

By part (ii) of Lemma S.11.1 and the update bound for $\theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) }$

$$
\left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) \right\| \leq \beta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } C _ { L } .
$$

Also, $\left\| u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) \right\| \leq C _ { P }$ and

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { n - 1 } | \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } - \beta _ { k - 1 } ^ { ( m ) } | \leq \beta _ { 0 } ^ { ( m ) } = \beta _ { n _ { m } } .
$$

Combining these estimates gives

$$
\left\| \sum _ { k = 0 } ^ { n - 1 } \beta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } \left( u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) - u _ { f } ^ { ( \theta _ { k } ^ { ( m ) } ) } ( Y _ { k + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } ) \right) \right\| \leq 3 C _ { P } \beta _ { n _ { m } } + C _ { L } \{ b ( n _ { m } ) - b ( n _ { m + 1 } ) \} = R _ { m } .
$$

## S.12 Strong monotonicity and exponential stability of projected ODEs

Definition S.12.1. Let $K \subset \mathbb { R } ^ { d }$ be a nonempty closed convex set and let $F : K  \mathbb { R } ^ { d }$ . Define

$$
\Pi _ { K } ( x , v ) : = \operatorname* { l i m } _ { h \downarrow 0 } \frac { \mathrm { p r o j } _ { K } ( x + h v ) - x } { h } , \qquad x \in K , \quad v \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d } ,
$$

and consider the projected dynamical system

$$
\dot { x } = \Pi _ { K } ( x , F ( x ) ) .
$$

The vector field F is strongly monotone at $x ^ { \star } \in K$ if there exists $\mu _ { \mathrm { s m } } > 0$ such that

$$
\langle F ( x ) - F ( x ^ { \star } ) , x - x ^ { \star } \rangle \leq - \mu _ { \mathrm { s m } } \| x - x ^ { \star } \| ^ { 2 } , \qquad x \in K .
$$
